None of its code is needed for H1/H3.\\nNEGATIVE results carried forward and NOT re-tested:\\n  - A*_h and D_ratio as headline bets (closed).\\n  - Raw co-occurrence growth indicators are CS-only.\\n  - Delta-rho over a strong B5 is insensitive, so H3 uses partial rho on O2r_resid instead.\\n  - Top-200-source truncation and per-experiment outcome tables were iteration-1 failure modes; the single scan removes both.\\nIf the run volume is not mounted, fall back as follows and log each deviation in deviations.json:\\n  - re-implement the analyser and rangefile from the definitions in this plan;\\n  - re-download the works manifest;\\n  - recompute the 1998-2002 backbone from the scan's per-year field co-assignment counts. It is then no longer the byte-identical frozen vector.\",\n  \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"GLOBAL RULES\\n- Python 3.12 + uv. Follow the aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks and aii-use-hardware skills. Seeds are fixed: 20260928, then 1, 2, 3.\\n- Every file goes inside the executor workspace.\\n- The OpenAlex key is read from env OPENALEX_API_KEY, taken from the run's original request. It is NEVER written to disk or to cache keys.\\n- Credit ledger: credits_log.csv. Hard cap 1,000 OpenAlex credits for this artifact. Check x-ratelimit-remaining first; skip the API steps if < 1,500 remain.\\n- OpenRouter cost ledger: llm_cost_log.csv. Sum usage.cost on every call. Hard stop at $2.00. On the first HTTP 403 'AI Inventor per-run OpenRouter budget', cancel all queued and in-flight calls and continue with the labels already obtained.\\n- Process control is PID-based only.\\n\\nFILES/MODULES\\n  common.py      field ids 11..36, field->group map, analyser (copied), paths\\n  rangefile.py   copied verbatim from art_yrradSC27HtQ\\n  lexicon.py     step 1\\n  prescreen.py   step 2\\n  scan_full.py   step 3\\n  grounding.py   step 4\\n  frame.py       step 5\\n  backbones.py   step 6\\n  features.py    step 7 (imports art_33 features.py functions)\\n  models.py      steps 8-10\\n  seal.py        sealing gate\\n  report.py      figures + method_out.json\\n  tests/         unit and regression tests\\n\\nSTEP 0. SCHEMA PROBE (5 min)\\n  meta = footer of works file 0 (rangefile tail read)\\n  Log every leaf path. Required: title, publication_year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, primary_location.source.id, topics.list.element.id, primary_topic.field.id (or derive it from primary_topic.id via topic_meta).\\n  Optional: concepts.list.element.id and concepts.list.element.score (the legacy tags). Set HAVE_TAGS = both present.\\n  Timing probe: read_columns on 5 files, with and without the concepts columns. Extrapolate the scan ETA over 2,040 files with 4 workers.\\n  If the ETA with tags is > 110 min, set HAVE_TAGS=False. The tags then become a sampled audit only (step 4c). Record the decision in deviations.json.\\n\\nSTEP 1. LEXICON (outcome-blind, frozen before any count is seen)\\n  Source: the snapshot legacy concepts entity (s3 data/parquet/concepts or data/concepts JSONL via the https://openalex.s3.amazonaws.com mirror).\\n  Fallback: cursor-page /concepts?per-page=200 with select=id,display_name,level,wikidata,description (about 330 calls; count the credits).\\n  Keep levels 2-5. Drop the level-0/1 names. Drop single tokens of <= 3 characters. Drop names that normalise to empty or to a stop word.\\n  Surface forms per concept: display_name; a hyphen/space variant; plural/singular s|es|ies variants; possessive stripped.\\n  Write lexicon_v0.parquet (concept_id, qid, name, level, description, forms[]). Hash it and record the hash in frozen_lexicon.sha256.\\n\\nSTEP 2. PRE-SCREEN ON A 1% FILE SAMPLE (about 20 random files, seed 20260928; files are partitioned by update date, so this is a quasi-random 1% sample of works)\\n  Build a pyahocorasick automaton over the normalised surface forms.\\n    Normalisation = common.normalise: lowercase, possessive strip, hyphen and slash -> space, collapse whitespace, pad the title with spaces.\\n  For each base work, run the AC search and keep only hits with word boundaries on both sides.\\n  Verify each hit with the stemmed positional matcher: phrase_spec(name) must be found by match_title-style logic on the title's analysed tokens.\\n  Aggregate sample hits per (concept, year).\\n  DROP concepts with >= 10 sampled verified hits in 1995-2002. At a 1% sample rate that means >= ~1,000 works in 8 years, so t0 >= 2003 is impossible.\\n  Do NOT drop on low counts: rare concepts must survive.\\n  Log the survivor count and the drop list (prescreen_dropped.csv).\\n  Expected: this removes the generic words that would otherwise flood the full scan.\\n  ALIASES for survivors: Wikidata wbgetentities (50 QIDs per call, free, polite 1 req/s, User-Agent set), English aliases.\\n    Drop aliases of <= 3 characters.\\n    Drop all-caps aliases of <= 5 characters. Acronyms are polysemous (the TAVI lesson). Keep an acronym only if the concept name itself is that acronym.\\n    Drop aliases that equal another concept's name or another concept's alias. An ambiguous alias goes to nobody.\\n    Drop aliases that the 1% sample shows with >= 10 hits in 1995-2002.\\n  Write lexicon_v1.parquet and hash it.\\n\\nSTEP 3. FULL ZERO-CREDIT SCAN (the only pass over the 2,040 files; run in background under aii-long-running-tasks; staged 3 -> 50 -> all files)\\n  Columns: the required columns (+ the tag columns if HAVE_TAGS). No id, referenced_works or authorships (see deviations).\\n  Worker init:\\n    AC automaton over lexicon_v1 forms -> concept index\\n    phrase specs\\n    source->venue-field LUT (sorted int64 source ids + np.searchsorted; unlabelled = 0; the 26 fields map to 1..26)\\n    topic->field LUT\\n    concept_id -> legacy id string map\\n  Per file:\\n    base mask = type in {article, review} & ~is_paratext & ~is_xpac & 1995 <= year <= 2022\\n    Aggregate A: G_total[year], plus venue-field totals VF[year, vfield] over base works (for O1 normalisation and field sizes).\\n    Aggregate B (backbones): for each base work, the SET of fields among its topics (distinct topics.field). For every unordered field pair and the diagonal, CO[year, i, j] += 1 (26x26 per year; the vectorised form of the iter-1 'works with topics in both fields' co-assignment).\\n    Title matching: AC hits -> boundary check -> stemmed verification. Each verified (row, concept) gets:\\n      vfield (0 = unlabelled)\\n      ptfield: primary_topic field\\n      tagstate in {1 = tag present with score >= 0.3, 2 = the work has tags but not this one (or score < 0.3), 3 = the work has no legacy tags, 0 = tags not read}\\n      mtype in {name_exact, name_variant, alias}\\n      single_token flag\\n    Aggregate C: sparse counts keyed (concept, year, vfield, ptfield, tagstate, mtype), via np.unique on an encoded int64 key.\\n    Reservoir R: for each concept, keep the matched titles with the 12 smallest hash(file_idx, row_idx) per era {<=2002, 2003-2014, 2015-2022}, together with year, vfield, tagstate and mtype. These are the samples for labelling.\\n    Rows U: for tagstate 3 hits (no legacy tags) in 1995-2022, store (concept, year, vfield, ptfield, title[:300], file, row). The sense filter needs them.\\n  Checkpoint every 100 files (reuse the ckpt pattern). Retry failed files on resume.\\n  Output: scan/agg_counts.parquet, scan/reservoir.parquet, scan/untagged_rows.parquet, scan/year_field_totals.npz, scan/co_by_year.npz\\n  Budget: if the 50-file stage extrapolates to > 150 min, drop ptfield (keep it only for the reservoir) and the tag columns, in that order. Log the change.\\n\\nSTEP 4. GROUNDING (all before any outcome column exists)\\n 4a Existing resources first.\\n    If HAVE_TAGS, rule TAG = title match & tagstate == 1.\\n    For biomedical concepts (home candidates BGM/Med/Immunology/Neuroscience/Pharmacology), note MeSH/PubTator3 as an optional precision cross-check. PubTator3 needs PMIDs, which this scan does not carry, so it is out of scope. Record it as not used.\\n 4b Benchmark (grounding_benchmark.csv, 400 pairs).\\n    Draw from reservoir R, stratified by home-field domain (Physical/Life/Health/Social), by mtype (exact/variant/alias), by single_token, and by tagstate (1/2/3).\\n    Labeller 1: a cheap instruction model chosen with aii-openrouter-llms. Criteria: price <= $0.15 per M input tokens and a JSON mode, e.g. a flash-lite or nano class model.\\n    The prompt gives the concept name, the Wikidata or legacy description and the title. The model answers {\\\"refers_to_concept\\\": true|false, \\\"confidence\\\": 0-1}.\\n    Batch 10 pairs per call.\\n    Labeller 2, from a different model family, labels 150 pairs. Report Cohen's kappa. If kappa < 0.6, adjudicate the disagreements with a third, stronger cheap model and log it.\\n    The executor itself reads 60 pairs (hand check) and records agreement with the labellers.\\n    Split 300 train / 100 test by concept. The train and test concepts are disjoint.\\n    Estimated cost: about 60 calls * 1.2k tokens, well under $0.10.\\n 4c Sense filter.\\n    Features:\\n      cosine(all-MiniLM-L6-v2(title), MiniLM(name + ': ' + description))\\n      single_token\\n      is_alias\\n      is_variant\\n      tagstate one-hot\\n      title length\\n      a capitalised-in-original flag\\n    Model: L2 logistic regression (C by 5-fold CV on train).\\n    Report on test: precision and recall of the rules (a) stemmed-any, (b) exact-name only, (c) TAG (if available), (d) filter p >= 0.5, and (e) TAG OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter). Save sense_filter.joblib and freeze it.\\n    Apply the filter to scan/untagged_rows only (CPU MiniLM; if there are more than 1.5M rows, embed a 20% hash sample and scale the counts, logged).\\n 4d Grounded counts per work class.\\n    If HAVE_TAGS: grounded = tagstate == 1, OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter pass).\\n    Else: grounded = all verified matches, and grounding precision rests on 4e alone.\\n    ALSO keep ungrounded verified counts as the 'match' variant, for the sensitivity analysis.\\n 4e Per-concept precision gate, applied to the concepts that pass the step-5 onset rule.\\n    LLM-label 10 grounded titles from the 2003-2022 reservoir, in 1 batched call.\\n    If 7-8 of 10 are positive, label 10 more.\\n    precision_c = positives / labelled.\\n    DROP if precision_c < 0.8.\\n    Expected: <= 5k concepts * ~700 tokens, about $0.3-0.6.\\n    Write grounding_precision.csv.\\n\\nSTEP 5. FRAME S1 (frame.py)\\n  Grounded yearly counts n_c[y] use all venues, unlabelled included.\\n  (t0, newborn) = art_33 onset() applied to n_c.\\n  KEEP 2003 <= t0 <= 2014 and sum n_c[t0..t0+2] >= 30. Newborn = False goes into a 're-emerging' stratum; it is kept in the primary analysis, and a newborn-only sensitivity is run.\\n  Gate on precision_c >= 0.8.\\n  HOME: take the first 30 grounded works that have a venue label, in year order. Randomise ties within a year by hash; the per-year counts decide how many come from each year. Home field(s) = fields with >= 40% of these works.\\n    If no field reaches 40%: home = the top field if its share is >= 25%, flagged 'weak_home'; otherwise drop as 'diffuse_born' and report the count.\\n    INTERSECTION stratum: >= 2 fields at >= 40%. The secondary flag intersect25 = second field >= 25%.\\n  GROUP = group of the dominant home field. Fixed map:\\n    DEV:\\n      CS = {17}\\n      Eng = {22}\\n      BGM = {13}\\n      Med = {27, 29 Nursing, 35 Dentistry, 36 Health Professions}\\n    HELD-OUT:\\n      PHYS = {15, 16, 19, 21, 25, 31}\\n      LIFEENV = {11, 23, 24, 28, 30, 34}\\n      SOC = {12, 14, 20, 32, 33}\\n      MATHDEC = {26, 18}\\n    split = DEV if the group is in DEV and t0 <= 2009; HELDOUT_GROUP if the group is held-out and t0 <= 2009; COHORT if t0 in 2010-2014 (all groups).\\n  EPISODES (c, j): j not in home(c), and n_early_cj = grounded venue-labelled works in j over t0..t0+2 >= 2.\\n    Columns: n_A (t0..t0+1), n_B (t0+2), share_early_j, growth_j.\\n  OUTCOME COUNTS: n_out_cj, share_out_j over t0+6..t0+8. SEALED for held-out and cohort rows (see seal.py).\\n  PRIMARY R_cj = [share_out_j >= 0.5*share_early_j AND n_out_cj >= 9]. This is the definition actually coded in art_33 method.py field_rows(), which produced the +0.10 lead. The direction's text says '>= 2 works', but that does not match the file it cites. The literal version is pre-registered as R_abs2 = [n_out_cj >= 2], with R_abs1 and R_abs3 as sensitivities. Log the discrepancy in deviations.json.\\n  ITERATION-1-EXACT subset: episodes with n_early >= 5 (sensitivity).\\n  Concept outcomes:\\n    O1: global-share rule, art_33 outcomes().\\n    O3: art_33 outcomes().\\n    O2r_m30 and O2r_m50: exact rarefaction over the venue-field counts of ALL grounded labelled works in t0+6..t0+8. No truncation. NA if there are < 30 labelled works.\\n    O2_raw.\\n    O2r_resid = O2r_m30 - (a + b*log N_outcome), with a and b fit on DEV only and frozen.\\n  Write frame_concepts.csv:\\n    concept_id, qid, name, level, aliases_used, t0, newborn, home, n_home, weak_home, intersect40, intersect25, group, split, precision_c, n_labelled_prec, label_coverage_early, tag_coverage, early_volume, in_P78 flag\\n  Write episodes.csv (outcome columns blank for sealed rows) and concept_outcomes.csv (same rule).\\n  TARGET: >= 400 concepts and >= 4,000 episodes.\\n    If short, relax in this order, logging each step: (i) early volume >= 20; (ii) add weak_home; (iii) include levels 1-5 minus discipline names.\\n    Stop relaxing as soon as the targets are met. All relaxations are outcome-blind and happen before freezing.\\n  API AUDIT (about 50 credits): 50 random frame concepts, one group_by=publication_year call each with filter title.search:\\\"<name>\\\",type:article|review,is_paratext:false. Report the Spearman correlation of log yearly counts, API vs snapshot match counts, and the median ratio. Also report t0 agreement with the iteration-1 S0 t0 for the P78 concepts in the frame (share with |dt0| <= 1).\\n\\nSTEP 6. BACKBONES (backbones.py)\\n  FROZEN gateway_j = field_backbone.json gateway_eig (1998-2002).\\n  From co_by_year, recompute PMI backbones for S0 = 1998-2002, S1 = 2003-07 and S2 = 2008-12:\\n    PMI_ij = log(C_ij * N / (n_i * n_j)), where N = base works with >= 1 topic in the slice\\n    phi = max(PMI, 0), zero diagonal\\n    eigenvector centrality (networkx, weight = phi), max-normalised\\n  CHECK: Spearman(recomputed S0 eig, frozen gateway_eig) >= 0.9. If it fails, report it; the frozen vector stays primary.\\n  Time-varying gateway_j,s: t0 2003-07 -> S0, t0 2008-12 -> S1, t0 2013-14 -> S2 (the slice that ends before t0).\\n  Report the within-field SD of gateway_j,s across slices. Low within-field variation means low FE power; say so.\\n  Log field size = log venue-labelled base works in j in the slice that ends before t0. The frozen primary uses n_field from the backbone JSON for the 1998-2002 slice.\\n  INSULARITY I_j (API, <= 600 credits):\\n    For each field j, 1 call of /works?filter=primary_location.source.id:<top-50 sources of j by 1998-2002 volume, OR-joined>,publication_year:1998-2002,type:article,referenced_works_count:>4&sample=100&seed=20260928&select=id,referenced_works.\\n    Take up to 10 random references per paper. Resolve them in 50-ID batches with select=id,primary_location. That is about 20 calls per field, about 550 credits in all.\\n    I_j = share of labelled references whose venue field = j.\\n    If the API is unavailable, set I_j = NA, drop it from the covariate set and log that.\\n  PLACEBO backbones: 200 degree-preserving rewirings of the frozen phi graph (seed k). Try nx.double_edge_swap with nswap = 10*E on the binary positive-phi graph, then reattach the original weights by permuting them within quintile strata of the endpoint degree product.\\n    If the graph is too dense for swaps (density > 0.8, or swap failures > 50%), permute the weights within the same strata on the fixed topology instead.\\n    Recompute the eigenvector centrality for each draw. Save placebo_gateways.npy (200 x 26).\\n\\nSTEP 7. FEATURES (features.py)\\n  Episode covariates:\\n    B5_c: log1p(early volume t0..t0+2), growth = log((n[t0+2]+1)/(n[t0]+1)), early off-home share, early venue entropy, early reach (fields with >= 2 works); all from grounded labelled works over t0..t0+2.\\n    log_field_size_j\\n    phi_home_j (mean phi over home fields)\\n    density_j: phi-weighted share of the other entered fields (>= 2 works by t0+2) among j's neighbours; the art_33 formula.\\n    P_j(-c): leave-concept-out retention propensity. Share of retained episodes among OTHER concepts' episodes in the same field j, same split set, |t0' - t0| <= 2.\\n      On DEV it uses dev episodes.\\n      On held-out it is computed only after unsealing, from held-out episodes.\\n      Variant P_j_train: the dev-only propensity, any cohort.\\n    I_j\\n    coverage: label_coverage_early_c, precision_c, tag_coverage_c\\n    log1p(n_early_cj), share_early_j, growth_j: the episode's own early size. These are always in the baseline; without them gateway could proxy episode size.\\n    gateway_j (frozen), gateway_j,s\\n  Concept-level H3 variants (art_33 g_family on early off-home labelled works):\\n    G = gateway_eig-weighted\\n    G_A = G on t0..t0+1 only (the iteration-1 window A)\\n    G_btw = betweenness-weighted\\n  Also compute (no selection, for iteration 3) the art_33 reference indicators on the common panel: count_indicators (log_count, share, growth, accel, burst), label_indicators, RS and DOM_*. Write concept_features_basic.csv.\\n\\nSTEP 8. DEV ANALYSIS, THEN FREEZE (models.py; dev rows only)\\n  FULL covariate set X0 = B5 + log_field_size + phi_home + density + P_j(-c) + I_j + coverage + log1p(n_early) + share_early + growth_j.\\n  X1 = X0 + gateway_j.\\n  Continuous covariates are standardised with dev means and SDs, which are frozen.\\n  PRIMARY: L2 logistic (C = 1.0, lbfgs), leave-one-home-group-out over the 4 dev groups. Metrics: pooled OOF AUC(X0), AUC(X1) and dAUC, plus per-group values.\\n    Concept-clustered REFIT bootstrap: 2,000 draws; resample concepts within group, refit, recompute dAUC on the OOF predictions.\\n  SECONDARY:\\n    (i) Conditional logit with concept FE (statsmodels ConditionalLogit, groups = concept). Covariates vary over j: gateway_j, log_field_size, phi_home, density, P_j, I_j, log1p(n_early), share_early. Report the beta_gateway z-test and an LR test for adding gateway; concepts with all-0 or all-1 outcomes drop out, so report n.\\n    (ii) LPM R ~ gateway_j,s + X0 (episode-varying parts) + field FE + t0-cohort FE, with SEs clustered by concept and two-way concept x field (linearmodels or manual CGM). beta_within > 0 is required.\\n    (iii) Boundary: X1 + gateway_j x top_tercile_home, where top_tercile_home = 1 if any home field is in the top tercile of frozen gateway_eig. The prediction is a negative interaction.\\n    (iv) Rival standard model: dAUC of adding {phi_home, density} to X0 minus those two plus gateway. That is, the relatedness pair versus gateway, each added to the same base. Paired bootstrap difference.\\n    (v) Placebo: for each of the 200 rewired gateway vectors, recompute the dev LOGO dAUC. The real dAUC must exceed the 95th percentile.\\n  FIELD-LEVEL ROBUSTNESS (added; gateway_j has only 26 values):\\n    leave-one-adopting-field-out dAUC (report min, max and the most influential field)\\n    a crossed pigeonhole bootstrap resampling concepts AND fields (Owen 2007), 500 draws, as a sensitivity CI\\n  POWER: simulate on dev. Plant gateway effects of size b in {0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3} SD log-odds with the dev covariate structure and the expected held-out n. Estimate P(dAUC CI > 0). Report the minimum detectable dAUC. This is NOT used to change the design.\\n  H3 on dev: partial Spearman of G, G_A and G_btw with O2r_resid given B5, reported once.\\n  FREEZE: write frozen_spec.json containing formulas, covariate list, standardisation constants, C, the R definitions, thresholds, the group map, the O2r_resid coefficients, the placebo seeds, the bootstrap seeds, the git hash of the code, sha256 of lexicon_v1 and sense_filter, and the list of held-out concept_ids.\\n    sha256(frozen_spec.json) -> logs/seal.log with a timestamp. Commit the workspace with git.\\n\\nSTEP 9. UNSEAL ONCE (seal.py enforces that frozen_spec.json exists and its sha matches seal.log; any second unseal call raises)\\n  Compute held-out and cohort outcome columns and P_j(-c) for held-out rows.\\n  Score the frozen models WITHOUT re-tuning.\\n  PRIMARY statistic: fit X0 and X1 on ALL dev episodes, predict held-out, compute dAUC.\\n    Refit bootstrap (2,000): each draw resamples dev concepts (refit) AND held-out concepts (evaluate). Output: 95% percentile CI.\\n    Per held-out group (PHYS, LIFEENV, SOC, MATHDEC) and COHORT: dAUC with a bootstrap SE.\\n    DerSimonian-Laird random-effects pooling across the 4 groups: pooled estimate, 95% CI, tau^2, I^2, and a sign test.\\n  Re-run on held-out, once each: (i) conditional logit, (ii) LPM field FE, (iii) boundary interaction, (iv) relatedness head-to-head, (v) placebo (200), and the field-level robustness checks.\\n  H3: in held-out (groups pooled; ranks computed within the pooled held-out set), partial Spearman of each G variant with O2r_resid given B5. Residualise ranks on B5 ranks by OLS.\\n    One-sided permutation p-value: 2,000 permutations of G within group.\\n    Holm across the 3 variants.\\n    Concept-bootstrap 95% CI. Per-group values and DL pooling. Report the same statistic for the rival concept-level relatedness summary REL_home.\\n  VERDICT H1 = CONFIRMED only if ALL of these hold:\\n    pooled dAUC >= 0.05 with refit CI > 0\\n    same sign in >= 3 of 4 held-out groups AND in COHORT\\n    beta_within(LPM field FE) > 0 with p < 0.05\\n    the real dAUC is NOT exceeded by... i.e. the placebo null: the real dAUC > the 95th percentile of placebo dAUCs\\n  Otherwise the verdict is PARTIAL or DISCONFIRMED, as in the hypothesis text. If gateway dies once P_j(-c) or field FE is added, write the 'trait of the adopting field' reading explicitly.\\n  SENSITIVITIES (reported, never used to change the verdict):\\n    R_abs1, R_abs2, R_abs3\\n    n_early >= 5 (iteration-1-exact)\\n    primary_topic fields instead of venue fields, for both features and outcomes\\n    newborn-only\\n    excluding intersection-born concepts\\n    'match' (ungrounded) counts\\n    P_j_train instead of P_j(-c)\\n    dropping I_j\\n    B5 on t0..t0+4 (the iteration-1 count window)\\n    gateway variants deg, btw, phimin, and the recomputed S0\\n\\nSTEP 10. OUTPUTS\\n  frame_concepts.csv, episodes.csv, concept_outcomes.csv: AUTHORITATIVE S1, all rows unsealed after step 9, with a split column.\\n  concept_features_basic.csv\\n  grounding_benchmark.csv, grounding_precision.csv, grounding_report.json (rule P/R, kappa, hand-check agreement)\\n  frozen_spec.json, logs/seal.log\\n  h1_results.json: dev and held-out, every model, CIs, per-group values, DL pooling, placebo percentile, robustness, sensitivities, power\\n  h3_results.json\\n  audit.json: API audit, P78 onset agreement, backbone recompute check, and the iteration-1 replication (fit the iteration-1 model on the frame's P78 subset: n_early >= 5, R_rel, B5 + log field size +/- gateway; compare with +0.10)\\n  deviations.json, credits_log.csv, llm_cost_log.csv\\n  figures/:\\n    forest plot of dAUC per group, cohort and pooled\\n    placebo histogram with the real value\\n    coefficient plot of the secondary models\\n    leave-one-field-out bars\\n    gateway map of the 26 fields\\n    PNG + PDF via aii-data-fig-gen style\\n  method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema (validate with aii-json):\\n    one example per episode: input = concept/field ids + covariates; output = R; predict_baseline / predict_gateway = OOF (dev) or frozen-model (held-out) probabilities\\n    metadata = headline numbers + verdicts\\n  full_, mini_ and preview_ variants\\n  reproducibility.md, README.md (with 'Restoring removed files'), .aii/manifest.yaml\\n    Manifest decisions:\\n      scan/ = keep. The 1-2 h scan is not reproducible bit-for-bit after a snapshot update.\\n      .venv, hf_cache, MiniLM cache = delete (redownloadable).\\n      snapshot metadata = delete (redownloadable, with the S3 URL).\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"1. Snapshot or range reads fail or are throttled.\\n   - Retry with backoff (rangefile already does this).\\n   - If more than 5% of files still fail after 3 resumes, analyse the completed files. Rescale the absolute thresholds (>= 20 onset, >= 30 early, >= 9 outcome) by the completed fraction, and log it.\\n   - The file-level partition is by update date, so a file subset is a quasi-random work sample. Never subset by field.\\n2. The scan ETA is too long on 4 vCPU. Cut in this order:\\n   - drop the legacy-tag columns (grounding then rests on the LLM precision gate plus the sense filter for all rows of gray-band concepts);\\n   - drop the per-hit primary_topic field (the P5-style sensitivity is then computed from the reservoir sample only);\\n   - raise the pre-screen drop threshold from 10 to 5 sampled pre-2003 hits.\\n   Each cut is logged. The analysis never scans only part of the years.\\n3. The legacy concepts entity is not in the snapshot.\\n   - Page /concepts through the API (about 330 list calls; check the credit cost on the first call and stop if it is > 1 credit per call and the remaining pool is < 3,000).\\n   - Otherwise use the Wikidata SPARQL endpoint: items with an OpenAlex/MAG concept ID property (P10283 or P6366), and their labels, descriptions and aliases. Log it.\\n4. It must match only at word boundaries.\\n  (c) Onset rule on synthetic yearly series: a newborn, a re-emerging series, and one crossing 20 in 2002 (excluded).\\n  (d) Home rule on synthetic first-30 lists, including ties and the intersection case.\\n  (e) Episode and R_rel on toy counts; hand-computed values must match.\\n  (f) The seal gate raises without frozen_spec.json, raises on a hash mismatch, and raises on a second unseal.\\n  (g) Planted positive control: add a synthetic field covariate that drives R. Write the result to audit.json.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"The design follows how relatedness and diversification studies in economic complexity and scientometrics are built and judged. What I read: the iteration-1 code and README of art_33_KKk_G8Gw5 and art_yrradSC27HtQ in the run tree; a search on Chinazzi et al. 2019 (EPJ Data Science 8:33, 'Mapping the physics research space'), which shows knowledge/relatedness density predicting a city's future specialisation in physics topics; and OpenAlex snapshot documentation (parquet snapshot since June 2026, schema mirrors the API). From prior knowledge I also rely on: Hidalgo et al. 2007 and 2018 (the principle of relatedness); Neffke, Henning & Boschma 2011 (Economic Geography: relatedness raises entry and lowers exit of industries in regions); Guevara et al. 2016 (Scientometrics research space); Alshamsi, Pinheiro & Hidalgo 2018 (Nature Communications: optimal diversification strategies, where the strategic value of an activity depends on its centrality in the relatedness network). Alshamsi et al. is the nearest neighbour to a 'centrality beats relatedness' claim and must be cited.\\n(1) BASELINES. Every entry/exit paper reports relatedness density as THE baseline predictor, usually with the actor's size, the activity's size or ubiquity, and actor and activity fixed effects. Exit and retention are modelled as the complement of entry: a linear probability model or logit of P(exit or keep | active at t) on density plus fixed effects. The first thing a reviewer names if missing is relatedness density and relatedness to origin, tuned by using the same pre-period backbone as the candidate. The second is fixed effects for the activity (here the adopting field). Without them, any field-level variable is confounded with 'this field keeps everything'.\\n(2) DATA. OpenAlex, MAG and WoS are the standard corpora. Known issues: OpenAlex coverage and document-type errors differ by field; venue labels are weak for conference-heavy CS and for non-English venues (iteration 1 saw Korean, Japanese and Russian engineering venues labelled Social Sciences); paper-level topic classifiers leak discipline from references. Legacy MAG concepts are deprecated but remain the only large Wikidata-linked concept vocabulary. Their selection condition (named by about 2019) is a known bias.\\n(3) CONTROLS. The pre-period network is frozen before the outcomes. Features and outcomes do not overlap in time. Actor or activity FE, plus a time-varying regressor, identify within-unit effects. Randomised networks preserve degrees or strengths (configuration-model nulls) to show that position, not degree or size, carries the signal. The most likely catch here: gateway_j is a FIELD attribute with only 26 values, so the effective sample for the gateway coefficient is 26 fields, not thousands of episodes. Reviewers in this area cluster by activity or two-way, and ask for leave-one-activity-out.\\n(4) HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Relatedness papers use thousands to millions of actor-activity observations and report AUC (entry prediction AUCs of about 0.6-0.8 are typical), standardised coefficients and clustered SEs. In scientometric prediction studies with a held-out design, a few hundred concepts per test set with bootstrap CIs is the norm, and n = 80 episodes from 28 concepts (iteration 1) is not believed. dAUC from nested logistic models is a known weak test (Pepe et al.; Vickers), so a coefficient or LR test is reported alongside it.\\n(5) MEASURES. AUC, dAUC, pseudo-R2, LR tests and marginal effects for entry and exit. Rarefied or volume-residualised breadth for diffusion. Random-effects meta-analysis across domains with I^2. Spearman or partial Spearman for concept-level associations. Results are reported per field or domain, and in pooled form.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(a) Relatedness baselines: phi(home, j) and relatedness density come from the same frozen 1998-2002 backbone as gateway_j. The head-to-head (relatedness pair vs gateway, each added to the same base) is an explicit pre-registered comparison.\\n(b) Activity (field) fixed effects with a time-varying regressor (gateway_j,s over 3 slices), a leave-concept-out field retention propensity, and concept fixed effects via conditional logit.\\n(c) A degree-preserving rewired-backbone placebo (200 draws) plus a permutation placebo.\\n(d) Temporal separation: features t0..t0+2, outcomes t0+6..t0+8, backbone before t0. Held-out groups and a later cohort, sealed by a hashed spec.\\n(e) Scale: >= 400 concepts and >= 4,000 episodes (about 50x iteration 1). A power simulation is reported.\\n(f) Reported measures: AUC, dAUC and coefficient or LR tests; per-group values plus DL pooling with I^2 and a sign test; no truncation of outcome windows. Size controls: log field size, the episode's own early size, and B5.\\n(g) The existing-resource-first grounding the user asked for: legacy tags, then an LLM-labelled benchmark with a trained sense filter.\\nCLOSED GAP (added beyond the direction): with only 26 fields, concept-clustered CIs alone overstate precision for a field-level regressor. The plan adds leave-one-adopting-field-out, a crossed concept x field bootstrap and two-way clustered SEs as robustness diagnostics. The pre-registered headline CI stays concept-clustered, as the hypothesis specifies. The field-level diagnostics are reported next to it, and a reader should weigh them.\\nDEPARTURES:\\n(1) Outcome definition. The primary R_cj is the relative-share definition actually coded in iteration 1, not the direction's literal '>= 2 works'; the literal one is a pre-registered sensitivity. Justification: this confirms the specific lead, and an absolute count mostly measures concept growth. Cost: the reader must accept a definition chosen before the outcomes were seen. Both are reported.\\n(2) Titles only, no abstracts: the scan budget does not allow abstracts. Iteration 1 found title matches cover about 48% of API volume (rho 0.88). Cost: lower recall and thinner small-field counts. Label coverage and early size are covariates.\\n(3) Venue labels come from primary_location.source rather than 'first non-repository location'. Repositories stay unlabelled. Minor.\\n(4) Insularity comes from a small API reference sample (100 papers x 10 refs per field) rather than a full citation pass. Cost: noisy I_j. It may be NA if the API is exhausted.\\n(5) Legacy vocabulary selection: concepts had to be named by about 2019. The episode-level test compares fields within a concept (concept FE), which neutralises this. The concept-level H3 carries it as a stated caveat. The outcome-blind Frame N (random-sample phrase mining) is NOT built here. The iteration-3 plan should, if concept-level base rates matter.\\n(6) No author-career outcome labels, lineage or candidate S (no referenced_works or authorships columns). Outcomes use venue fields, with primary_topic as a sensitivity. Cost: outcome and feature labels share a system. The P5-style primary_topic rerun partly addresses this.\\n(7) Per-concept precision rests on 10-20 LLM-labelled titles. That is a noisy gate, but it is applied outcome-blind and benchmark-validated. Nobody in this field hand-labels thousands of concepts. Precision_c is kept as a covariate.\\n(8) Health professions, nursing and dentistry are assigned to the DEV Medicine group, and veterinary to life/environment. Record it as not used.\\n 4b Benchmark (grounding_benchmark.csv, 400 pairs).\\n    Draw from reservoir R, stratified by home-field domain (Physical/Life/Health/Social), by mtype (exact/variant/alias), by single_token, and by tagstate (1/2/3).\\n    Labeller 1: a cheap instruction model chosen with aii-openrouter-llms. Criteria: price <= $0.15 per M input tokens and a JSON mode, e.g. a flash-lite or nano class model.\\n    The prompt gives the concept name, the Wikidata or legacy description and the title. The model answers {\\\"refers_to_concept\\\": true|false, \\\"confidence\\\": 0-1}.\\n    Batch 10 pairs per call.\\n    Labeller 2, from a different model family, labels 150 pairs. Report Cohen's kappa. If kappa < 0.6, adjudicate the disagreements with a third, stronger cheap model and log it.\\n    The executor itself reads 60 pairs (hand check) and records agreement with the labellers.\\n    Split 300 train / 100 test by concept. The train and test concepts are disjoint.\\n    Estimated cost: about 60 calls * 1.2k tokens, well under $0.10.\\n 4c Sense filter.\\n    Features:\\n      cosine(all-MiniLM-L6-v2(title), MiniLM(name + ': ' + description))\\n      single_token\\n      is_alias\\n      is_variant\\n      tagstate one-hot\\n      title length\\n      a capitalised-in-original flag\\n    Model: L2 logistic regression (C by 5-fold CV on train).\\n    Report on test: precision and recall of the rules (a) stemmed-any, (b) exact-name only, (c) TAG (if available), (d) filter p >= 0.5, and (e) TAG OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter). Save sense_filter.joblib and freeze it.\\n    Apply the filter to scan/untagged_rows only (CPU MiniLM; if there are more than 1.5M rows, embed a 20% hash sample and scale the counts, logged).\\n 4d Grounded counts per work class.\\n    If HAVE_TAGS: grounded = tagstate == 1, OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter pass).\\n    Else: grounded = all verified matches, and grounding precision rests on 4e alone.\\n    ALSO keep ungrounded verified counts as the 'match' variant, for the sensitivity analysis.\\n 4e Per-concept precision gate, applied to the concepts that pass the step-5 onset rule.\\n    LLM-label 10 grounded titles from the 2003-2022 reservoir, in 1 batched call.\\n    If 7-8 of 10 are positive, label 10 more.\\n    precision_c = positives / labelled.\\n    DROP if precision_c < 0.8.\\n    Expected: <= 5k concepts * ~700 tokens, about $0.3-0.6.\\n    Write grounding_precision.csv.\\n\\nSTEP 5. FRAME S1 (frame.py)\\n  Grounded yearly counts n_c[y] use all venues, unlabelled included.\\n  (t0, newborn) = art_33 onset() applied to n_c.\\n  KEEP 2003 <= t0 <= 2014 and sum n_c[t0..t0+2] >= 30. Newborn = False goes into a 're-emerging' stratum; it is kept in the primary analysis, and a newborn-only sensitivity is run.\\n  Gate on precision_c >= 0.8.\\n  HOME: take the first 30 grounded works that have a venue label, in year order. Randomise ties within a year by hash; the per-year counts decide how many come from each year. Home field(s) = fields with >= 40% of these works.\\n    If no field reaches 40%: home = the top field if its share is >= 25%, flagged 'weak_home'; otherwise drop as 'diffuse_born' and report the count.\\n    INTERSECTION stratum: >= 2 fields at >= 40%. The secondary flag intersect25 = second field >= 25%.\\n  GROUP = group of the dominant home field. Fixed map:\\n    DEV:\\n      CS = {17}\\n      Eng = {22}\\n      BGM = {13}\\n      Med = {27, 29 Nursing, 35 Dentistry, 36 Health Professions}\\n    HELD-OUT:\\n      PHYS = {15, 16, 19, 21, 25, 31}\\n      LIFEENV = {11, 23, 24, 28, 30, 34}\\n      SOC = {12, 14, 20, 32, 33}\\n      MATHDEC = {26, 18}\\n    split = DEV if the group is in DEV and t0 <= 2009; HELDOUT_GROUP if the group is held-out and t0 <= 2009; COHORT if t0 in 2010-2014 (all groups).\\n  EPISODES (c, j): j not in home(c), and n_early_cj = grounded venue-labelled works in j over t0..t0+2 >= 2.\\n    Columns: n_A (t0..t0+1), n_B (t0+2), share_early_j, growth_j.\\n  OUTCOME COUNTS: n_out_cj, share_out_j over t0+6..t0+8. SEALED for held-out and cohort rows (see seal.py).\\n  PRIMARY R_cj = [share_out_j >= 0.5*share_early_j AND n_out_cj >= 9]. This is the definition actually coded in art_33 method.py field_rows(), which produced the +0.10 lead. The direction's text says '>= 2 works', but that does not match the file it cites. The literal version is pre-registered as R_abs2 = [n_out_cj >= 2], with R_abs1 and R_abs3 as sensitivities. Log the discrepancy in deviations.json.\\n  ITERATION-1-EXACT subset: episodes with n_early >= 5 (sensitivity).\\n  Concept outcomes:\\n    O1: global-share rule, art_33 outcomes().\\n    O3: art_33 outcomes().\\n    O2r_m30 and O2r_m50: exact rarefaction over the venue-field counts of ALL grounded labelled works in t0+6..t0+8. No truncation. NA if there are < 30 labelled works.\\n    O2_raw.\\n    O2r_resid = O2r_m30 - (a + b*log N_outcome), with a and b fit on DEV only and frozen.\\n  Write frame_concepts.csv:\\n    concept_id, qid, name, level, aliases_used, t0, newborn, home, n_home, weak_home, intersect40, intersect25, group, split, precision_c, n_labelled_prec, label_coverage_early, tag_coverage, early_volume, in_P78 flag\\n  Write episodes.csv (outcome columns blank for sealed rows) and concept_outcomes.csv (same rule).\\n  TARGET: >= 400 concepts and >= 4,000 episodes.\\n    If short, relax in this order, logging each step: (i) early volume >= 20; (ii) add weak_home; (iii) include levels 1-5 minus discipline names.\\n    Stop relaxing as soon as the targets are met. All relaxations are outcome-blind and happen before freezing.\\n  API AUDIT (about 50 credits): 50 random frame concepts, one group_by=publication_year call each with filter title.search:\\\"<name>\\\",type:article|review,is_paratext:false. Report the Spearman correlation of log yearly counts, API vs snapshot match counts, and the median ratio. Also report t0 agreement with the iteration-1 S0 t0 for the P78 concepts in the frame (share with |dt0| <= 1).\\n\\nSTEP 6. BACKBONES (backbones.py)\\n  FROZEN gateway_j = field_backbone.json gateway_eig (1998-2002).\\n  From co_by_year, recompute PMI backbones for S0 = 1998-2002, S1 = 2003-07 and S2 = 2008-12:\\n    PMI_ij = log(C_ij * N / (n_i * n_j)), where N = base works with >= 1 topic in the slice\\n    phi = max(PMI, 0), zero diagonal\\n    eigenvector centrality (networkx, weight = phi), max-normalised\\n  CHECK: Spearman(recomputed S0 eig, frozen gateway_eig) >= 0.9. If it fails, report it; the frozen vector stays primary.\\n  Time-varying gateway_j,s: t0 2003-07 -> S0, t0 2008-12 -> S1, t0 2013-14 -> S2 (the slice that ends before t0).\\n  Report the within-field SD of gateway_j,s across slices. Low within-field variation means low FE power; say so.\\n  Log field size = log venue-labelled base works in j in the slice that ends before t0. The frozen primary uses n_field from the backbone JSON for the 1998-2002 slice.\\n  INSULARITY I_j (API, <= 600 credits):\\n    For each field j, 1 call of /works?filter=primary_location.source.id:<top-50 sources of j by 1998-2002 volume, OR-joined>,publication_year:1998-2002,type:article,referenced_works_count:>4&sample=100&seed=20260928&select=id,referenced_works.\\n    Take up to 10 random references per paper. Resolve them in 50-ID batches with select=id,primary_location. That is about 20 calls per field, about 550 credits in all.\\n    I_j = share of labelled references whose venue field = j.\\n    If the API is unavailable, set I_j = NA, drop it from the covariate set and log that.\\n  PLACEBO backbones: 200 degree-preserving rewirings of the frozen phi graph (seed k). Try nx.double_edge_swap with nswap = 10*E on the binary positive-phi graph, then reattach the original weights by permuting them within quintile strata of the endpoint degree product.\\n    If the graph is too dense for swaps (density > 0.8, or swap failures > 50%), permute the weights within the same strata on the fixed topology instead.\\n    Recompute the eigenvector centrality for each draw. Save placebo_gateways.npy (200 x 26).\\n\\nSTEP 7. FEATURES (features.py)\\n  Episode covariates:\\n    B5_c: log1p(early volume t0..t0+2), growth = log((n[t0+2]+1)/(n[t0]+1)), early off-home share, early venue entropy, early reach (fields with >= 2 works); all from grounded labelled works over t0..t0+2.\\n    log_field_size_j\\n    phi_home_j (mean phi over home fields)\\n    density_j: phi-weighted share of the other entered fields (>= 2 works by t0+2) among j's neighbours; the art_33 formula.\\n    P_j(-c): leave-concept-out retention propensity. Share of retained episodes among OTHER concepts' episodes in the same field j, same split set, |t0' - t0| <= 2.\\n      On DEV it uses dev episodes.\\n      On held-out it is computed only after unsealing, from held-out episodes.\\n      Variant P_j_train: the dev-only propensity, any cohort.\\n    I_j\\n    coverage: label_coverage_early_c, precision_c, tag_coverage_c\\n    log1p(n_early_cj), share_early_j, growth_j: the episode's own early size. These are always in the baseline; without them gateway could proxy episode size.\\n    gateway_j (frozen), gateway_j,s\\n  Concept-level H3 variants (art_33 g_family on early off-home labelled works):\\n    G = gateway_eig-weighted\\n    G_A = G on t0..t0+1 only (the iteration-1 window A)\\n    G_btw = betweenness-weighted\\n  Also compute (no selection, for iteration 3) the art_33 reference indicators on the common panel: count_indicators (log_count, share, growth, accel, burst), label_indicators, RS and DOM_*. Write concept_features_basic.csv.\\n\\nSTEP 8. DEV ANALYSIS, THEN FREEZE (models.py; dev rows only)\\n  FULL covariate set X0 = B5 + log_field_size + phi_home + density + P_j(-c) + I_j + coverage + log1p(n_early) + share_early + growth_j.\\n  X1 = X0 + gateway_j.\\n  Continuous covariates are standardised with dev means and SDs, which are frozen.\\n  PRIMARY: L2 logistic (C = 1.0, lbfgs), leave-one-home-group-out over the 4 dev groups. Metrics: pooled OOF AUC(X0), AUC(X1) and dAUC, plus per-group values.\\n    Concept-clustered REFIT bootstrap: 2,000 draws; resample concepts within group, refit, recompute dAUC on the OOF predictions.\\n  SECONDARY:\\n    (i) Conditional logit with concept FE (statsmodels ConditionalLogit, groups = concept). Covariates vary over j: gateway_j, log_field_size, phi_home, density, P_j, I_j, log1p(n_early), share_early. Report the beta_gateway z-test and an LR test for adding gateway; concepts with all-0 or all-1 outcomes drop out, so report n.\\n    (ii) LPM R ~ gateway_j,s + X0 (episode-varying parts) + field FE + t0-cohort FE, with SEs clustered by concept and two-way concept x field (linearmodels or manual CGM). beta_within > 0 is required.\\n    (iii) Boundary: X1 + gateway_j x top_tercile_home, where top_tercile_home = 1 if any home field is in the top tercile of frozen gateway_eig. The prediction is a negative interaction.\\n    (iv) Rival standard model: dAUC of adding {phi_home, density} to X0 minus those two plus gateway. That is, the relatedness pair versus gateway, each added to the same base. Paired bootstrap difference.\\n    (v) Placebo: for each of the 200 rewired gateway vectors, recompute the dev LOGO dAUC. The real dAUC must exceed the 95th percentile.\\n  FIELD-LEVEL ROBUSTNESS (added; gateway_j has only 26 values):\\n    leave-one-adopting-field-out dAUC (report min, max and the most influential field)\\n    a crossed pigeonhole bootstrap resampling concepts AND fields (Owen 2007), 500 draws, as a sensitivity CI\\n  POWER: simulate on dev. Plant gateway effects of size b in {0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3} SD log-odds with the dev covariate structure and the expected held-out n. Estimate P(dAUC CI > 0). Report the minimum detectable dAUC. This is NOT used to change the design.\\n  H3 on dev: partial Spearman of G, G_A and G_btw with O2r_resid given B5, reported once.\\n  FREEZE: write frozen_spec.json containing formulas, covariate list, standardisation constants, C, the R definitions, thresholds, the group map, the O2r_resid coefficients, the placebo seeds, the bootstrap seeds, the git hash of the code, sha256 of lexicon_v1 and sense_filter, and the list of held-out concept_ids.\\n    sha256(frozen_spec.json) -> logs/seal.log with a timestamp. Commit the workspace with git.\\n\\nSTEP 9. UNSEAL ONCE (seal.py enforces that frozen_spec.json exists and its sha matches seal.log; any second unseal call raises)\\n  Compute held-out and cohort outcome columns and P_j(-c) for held-out rows.\\n  Score the frozen models WITHOUT re-tuning.\\n  PRIMARY statistic: fit X0 and X1 on ALL dev episodes, predict held-out, compute dAUC.\\n    Refit bootstrap (2,000): each draw resamples dev concepts (refit) AND held-out concepts (evaluate). Output: 95% percentile CI.\\n    Per held-out group (PHYS, LIFEENV, SOC, MATHDEC) and COHORT: dAUC with a bootstrap SE.\\n    DerSimonian-Laird random-effects pooling across the 4 groups: pooled estimate, 95% CI, tau^2, I^2, and a sign test.\\n  Re-run on held-out, once each: (i) conditional logit, (ii) LPM field FE, (iii) boundary interaction, (iv) relatedness head-to-head, (v) placebo (200), and the field-level robustness checks.\\n  H3: in held-out (groups pooled; ranks computed within the pooled held-out set), partial Spearman of each G variant with O2r_resid given B5. Residualise ranks on B5 ranks by OLS.\\n    One-sided permutation p-value: 2,000 permutations of G within group.\\n    Holm across the 3 variants.\\n    Concept-bootstrap 95% CI. Per-group values and DL pooling. Report the same statistic for the rival concept-level relatedness summary REL_home.\\n  VERDICT H1 = CONFIRMED only if ALL of these hold:\\n    pooled dAUC >= 0.05 with refit CI > 0\\n    same sign in >= 3 of 4 held-out groups AND in COHORT\\n    beta_within(LPM field FE) > 0 with p < 0.05\\n    the real dAUC is NOT exceeded by... i.e. the placebo null: the real dAUC > the 95th percentile of placebo dAUCs\\n  Otherwise the verdict is PARTIAL or DISCONFIRMED, as in the hypothesis text. If gateway dies once P_j(-c) or field FE is added, write the 'trait of the adopting field' reading explicitly.\\n  SENSITIVITIES (reported, never used to change the verdict):\\n    R_abs1, R_abs2, R_abs3\\n    n_early >= 5 (iteration-1-exact)\\n    primary_topic fields instead of venue fields, for both features and outcomes\\n    newborn-only\\n    excluding intersection-born concepts\\n    'match' (ungrounded) counts\\n    P_j_train instead of P_j(-c)\\n    dropping I_j\\n    B5 on t0..t0+4 (the iteration-1 count window)\\n    gateway variants deg, btw, phimin, and the recomputed S0\\n\\nSTEP 10. OUTPUTS\\n  frame_concepts.csv, episodes.csv, concept_outcomes.csv: AUTHORITATIVE S1, all rows unsealed after step 9, with a split column.\\n  concept_features_basic.csv\\n  grounding_benchmark.csv, grounding_precision.csv, grounding_report.json (rule P/R, kappa, hand-check agreement)\\n  frozen_spec.json, logs/seal.log\\n  h1_results.json: dev and held-out, every model, CIs, per-group values, DL pooling, placebo percentile, robustness, sensitivities, power\\n  h3_results.json\\n  audit.json: API audit, P78 onset agreement, backbone recompute check, and the iteration-1 replication (fit the iteration-1 model on the frame's P78 subset: n_early >= 5, R_rel, B5 + log field size +/- gateway; compare with +0.10)\\n  deviations.json, credits_log.csv, llm_cost_log.csv\\n  figures/:\\n    forest plot of dAUC per group, cohort and pooled\\n    placebo histogram with the real value\\n    coefficient plot of the secondary models\\n    leave-one-field-out bars\\n    gateway map of the 26 fields\\n    PNG + PDF via aii-data-fig-gen style\\n  method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema (validate with aii-json):\\n    one example per episode: input = concept/field ids + covariates; output = R; predict_baseline / predict_gateway = OOF (dev) or frozen-model (held-out) probabilities\\n    metadata = headline numbers + verdicts\\n  full_, mini_ and preview_ variants\\n  reproducibility.md, README.md (with 'Restoring removed files'), .aii/manifest.yaml\\n    Manifest decisions:\\n      scan/ = keep. The 1-2 h scan is not reproducible bit-for-bit after a snapshot update.\\n      .venv, hf_cache, MiniLM cache = delete (redownloadable).\\n      snapshot metadata = delete (redownloadable, with the S3 URL).\",", "+  \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"GLOBAL RULES\\n- Python 3.12 + uv. Follow the aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks and aii-use-hardware skills. Seeds are fixed: 20260928, then 1, 2, 3.\\n- Every file goes inside the executor workspace.\\n- The OpenAlex key is read from env OPENALEX_API_KEY, taken from the run's original request. It is NEVER written to disk or to cache keys.\\n- Credit ledger: credits_log.csv. Hard cap 1,000 OpenAlex credits for this artifact. Check x-ratelimit-remaining first; skip the API steps if < 1,500 remain.\\n- OpenRouter cost ledger: llm_cost_log.csv. Sum usage.cost on every call. Hard stop at $2.00. On the first HTTP 403 'AI Inventor per-run OpenRouter budget', cancel all queued and in-flight calls and continue with the labels already obtained.\\n- Process control is PID-based only.\\n\\nFILES/MODULES\\n  common.py      field ids 11..36, field->group map, analyser (copied), paths\\n  rangefile.py   copied verbatim from art_yrradSC27HtQ\\n  lexicon.py     step 1\\n  prescreen.py   step 2\\n  scan_full.py   step 3\\n  grounding.py   step 4\\n  frame.py       step 5\\n  backbones.py   step 6\\n  features.py    step 7 (imports art_33 features.py functions)\\n  models.py      steps 8-10\\n  seal.py        sealing gate\\n  report.py      figures + method_out.json\\n  tests/         unit and regression tests\\n\\nSTEP 0. SCHEMA PROBE (5 min)\\n  meta = footer of works file 0 (rangefile tail read)\\n  Log every leaf path. Required: title, publication_year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, primary_location.source.id, topics.list.element.id, primary_topic.field.id (or derive it from primary_topic.id via topic_meta).\\n  Optional: concepts.list.element.id and concepts.list.element.score (the legacy tags). Set HAVE_TAGS = both present.\\n  Timing probe: read_columns on 5 files, with and without the concepts columns. Extrapolate the scan ETA over 2,040 files with 4 workers.\\n  If the ETA with tags is > 110 min, set HAVE_TAGS=False. The tags then become a sampled audit only (step 4c). Record the decision in deviations.json.\\n\\nSTEP 1. LEXICON (outcome-blind, frozen before any count is seen)\\n  Source: the snapshot legacy concepts entity (s3 data/parquet/concepts or data/concepts JSONL via the https://openalex.s3.amazonaws.com mirror).\\n  Fallback: cursor-page /concepts?per-page=200 with select=id,display_name,level,wikidata,description (about 330 calls; count the credits).\\n  Keep levels 2-5. Drop the level-0/1 names. Drop single tokens of <= 3 characters. Drop names that normalise to empty or to a stop word.\\n  Surface forms per concept: display_name; a hyphen/space variant; plural/singular s|es|ies variants; possessive stripped.\\n  Write lexicon_v0.parquet (concept_id, qid, name, level, description, forms[]). Hash it and record the hash in frozen_lexicon.sha256.\\n\\nSTEP 2. PRE-SCREEN ON A 1% FILE SAMPLE (about 20 random files, seed 20260928; files are partitioned by update date, so this is a quasi-random 1% sample of works)\\n  Build a pyahocorasick automaton over the normalised surface forms.\\n    Normalisation = common.normalise: lowercase, possessive strip, hyphen and slash -> space, collapse whitespace, pad the title with spaces.\\n  For each base work, run the AC search and keep only hits with word boundaries on both sides.\\n  Verify each hit with the stemmed positional matcher: phrase_spec(name) must be found by match_title-style logic on the title's analysed tokens.\\n  Aggregate sample hits per (concept, year).\\n  DROP concepts with >= 10 sampled verified hits in 1995-2002. At a 1% sample rate that means >= ~1,000 works in 8 years, so t0 >= 2003 is impossible.\\n  Do NOT drop on low counts: rare concepts must survive.\\n  Log the survivor count and the drop list (prescreen_dropped.csv).\\n  Expected: this removes the generic words that would otherwise flood the full scan.\\n  ALIASES for survivors: Wikidata wbgetentities (50 QIDs per call, free, polite 1 req/s, User-Agent set), English aliases.\\n    Drop aliases of <= 3 characters.\\n    Drop all-caps aliases of <= 5 characters. Acronyms are polysemous (the TAVI lesson). Keep an acronym only if the concept name itself is that acronym.\\n    Drop aliases that equal another concept's name or another concept's alias. An ambiguous alias goes to nobody.\\n    Drop aliases that the 1% sample shows with >= 10 hits in 1995-2002.\\n  Write lexicon_v1.parquet and hash it.\\n\\nSTEP 3. FULL ZERO-CREDIT SCAN (the only pass over the 2,040 files; run in background under aii-long-running-tasks; staged 3 -> 50 -> all files)\\n  Columns: the required columns (+ the tag columns if HAVE_TAGS). No id, referenced_works or authorships (see deviations).\\n  Worker init:\\n    AC automaton over lexicon_v1 forms -> concept index\\n    phrase specs\\n    source->venue-field LUT (sorted int64 source ids + np.searchsorted; unlabelled = 0; the 26 fields map to 1..26)\\n    topic->field LUT\\n    concept_id -> legacy id string map\\n  Per file:\\n    base mask = type in {article, review} & ~is_paratext & ~is_xpac & 1995 <= year <= 2022\\n    Aggregate A: G_total[year], plus venue-field totals VF[year, vfield] over base works (for O1 normalisation and field sizes).\\n    Aggregate B (backbones): for each base work, the SET of fields among its topics (distinct topics.field). For every unordered field pair and the diagonal, CO[year, i, j] += 1 (26x26 per year; the vectorised form of the iter-1 'works with topics in both fields' co-assignment).\\n    Title matching: AC hits -> boundary check -> stemmed verification. Each verified (row, concept) gets:\\n      vfield (0 = unlabelled)\\n      ptfield: primary_topic field\\n      tagstate in {1 = tag present with score >= 0.3, 2 = the work has tags but not this one (or score < 0.3), 3 = the work has no legacy tags, 0 = tags not read}\\n      mtype in {name_exact, name_variant, alias}\\n      single_token flag\\n    Aggregate C: sparse counts keyed (concept, year, vfield, ptfield, tagstate, mtype), via np.unique on an encoded int64 key.\\n    Reservoir R: for each concept, keep the matched titles with the 12 smallest hash(file_idx, row_idx) per era {<=2002, 2003-2014, 2015-2022}, together with year, vfield, tagstate and mtype. These are the samples for labelling.\\n    Rows U: for tagstate 3 hits (no legacy tags) in 1995-2022, store (concept, year, vfield, ptfield, title[:300], file, row). The sense filter needs them.\\n  Checkpoint every 100 files (reuse the ckpt pattern). Retry failed files on resume.\\n  Output: scan/agg_counts.parquet, scan/reservoir.parquet, scan/untagged_rows.parquet, scan/year_field_totals.npz, scan/co_by_year.npz\\n  Budget: if the 50-file stage extrapolates to > 150 min, drop ptfield (keep it only for the reservoir) and the tag columns, in that order. Log the change.\\n\\nSTEP 4. GROUNDING (all before any outcome column exists)\\n 4a Existing resources first.\\n    If HAVE_TAGS, rule TAG = title match & tagstate == 1.\\n    For biomedical concepts (home candidates BGM/Med/Immunology/Neuroscience/Pharmacology), note MeSH/PubTator3 as an optional precision cross-check. PubTator3 needs PMIDs, which this scan does not carry, so it is out of scope. Record it as not used.\\n 4b Benchmark (grounding_benchmark.csv, 400 pairs).\\n    Draw from reservoir R, stratified by home-field domain (Physical/Life/Health/Social), by mtype (exact/variant/alias), by single_token, and by tagstate (1/2/3).\\n    Labeller 1: a cheap instruction model chosen with aii-openrouter-llms. Criteria: price <= $0.15 per M input tokens and a JSON mode, e.g. a flash-lite or nano class model.\\n    The prompt gives the concept name, the Wikidata or legacy description and the title. The model answers {\\\"refers_to_concept\\\": true|false, \\\"confidence\\\": 0-1}.\\n    Batch 10 pairs per call.\\n    Labeller 2, from a different model family, labels 150 pairs. Report Cohen's kappa. If kappa < 0.6, adjudicate the disagreements with a third, stronger cheap model and log it.\\n    The executor itself reads 60 pairs (hand check) and records agreement with the labellers.\\n    Split 300 train / 100 test by concept. The train and test concepts are disjoint.\\n    Estimated cost: about 60 calls * 1.2k tokens, well under $0.10.\\n 4c Sense filter.\\n    Features:\\n      cosine(all-MiniLM-L6-v2(title), MiniLM(name + ': ' + description))\\n      single_token\\n      is_alias\\n      is_variant\\n      tagstate one-hot\\n      title length\\n      a capitalised-in-original flag\\n    Model: L2 logistic regression (C by 5-fold CV on train).\\n    Report on test: precision and recall of the rules (a) stemmed-any, (b) exact-name only, (c) TAG (if available), (d) filter p >= 0.5, and (e) TAG OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter). Save sense_filter.joblib and freeze it.\\n    Apply the filter to scan/untagged_rows only (CPU MiniLM; if there are more than 1.5M rows, embed a 20% hash sample and scale the counts, logged).\\n 4d Grounded counts per work class.\\n    If HAVE_TAGS: grounded = tagstate == 1, OR (tagstate == 3 AND filter pass).\\n    Else: grounded = all verified matches, and grounding precision rests on 4e alone.\\n    ALSO keep ungrounded verified counts as the 'match' variant, for the sensitivity analysis.\\n 4e Per-concept precision gate, applied to the concepts that pass the step-5 onset rule.\\n    LLM-label 10 grounded titles from the 2003-2022 reservoir, in 1 batched call.\\n    If 7-8 of 10 are positive, label 10 more.\\n    precision_c = positives / labelled.\\n    DROP if precision_c < 0.8.\\n    Expected: <= 5k concepts * ~700 tokens, about $0.3-0.6.\\n    Write grounding_precision.csv.\\n\\nSTEP 5. FRAME S1 (frame.py)\\n  Grounded yearly counts n_c[y] use all venues, unlabelled included.\\n  (t0, newborn) = art_33 onset() applied to n_c.\\n  KEEP 2003 <= t0 <= 2014 and sum n_c[t0..t0+2] >= 30. Write the result to audit.json.\",", "   \"domain_practice\": \"The design follows how relatedness and diversification studies in economic complexity and scientometrics are built and judged. What I read: the iteration-1 code and README of art_33_KKk_G8Gw5 and art_yrradSC27HtQ in the run tree; a search on Chinazzi et al. 2019 (EPJ Data Science 8:33, 'Mapping the physics research space'), which shows knowledge/relatedness density predicting a city's future specialisation in physics topics; and OpenAlex snapshot documentation (parquet snapshot since June 2026, schema mirrors the API). From prior knowledge I also rely on: Hidalgo et al. 2007 and 2018 (the principle of relatedness); Neffke, Henning & Boschma 2011 (Economic Geography: relatedness raises entry and lowers exit of industries in regions); Guevara et al. 2016 (Scientometrics research space); Alshamsi, Pinheiro & Hidalgo 2018 (Nature Communications: optimal diversification strategies, where the strategic value of an activity depends on its centrality in the relatedness network). Alshamsi et al. is the nearest neighbour to a 'centrality beats relatedness' claim and must be cited.\\n(1) BASELINES. Every entry/exit paper reports relatedness density as THE baseline predictor, usually with the actor's size, the activity's size or ubiquity, and actor and activity fixed effects. Exit and retention are modelled as the complement of entry: a linear probability model or logit of P(exit or keep | active at t) on density plus fixed effects. The first thing a reviewer names if missing is relatedness density and relatedness to origin, tuned by using the same pre-period backbone as the candidate. The second is fixed effects for the activity (here the adopting field). Without them, any field-level variable is confounded with 'this field keeps everything'.\\n(2) DATA. OpenAlex, MAG and WoS are the standard corpora. Known issues: OpenAlex coverage and document-type errors differ by field; venue labels are weak for conference-heavy CS and for non-English venues (iteration 1 saw Korean, Japanese and Russian engineering venues labelled Social Sciences); paper-level topic classifiers leak discipline from references. Legacy MAG concepts are deprecated but remain the only large Wikidata-linked concept vocabulary. Their selection condition (named by about 2019) is a known bias.\\n(3) CONTROLS. The pre-period network is frozen before the outcomes. Features and outcomes do not overlap in time. Actor or activity FE, plus a time-varying regressor, identify within-unit effects. Randomised networks preserve degrees or strengths (configuration-model nulls) to show that position, not degree or size, carries the signal. The most likely catch here: gateway_j is a FIELD attribute with only 26 values, so the effective sample for the gateway coefficient is 26 fields, not thousands of episodes. Reviewers in this area cluster by activity or two-way, and ask for leave-one-activity-out.\\n(4) HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Relatedness papers use thousands to millions of actor-activity observations and report AUC (entry prediction AUCs of about 0.6-0.8 are typical), standardised coefficients and clustered SEs. In scientometric prediction studies with a held-out design, a few hundred concepts per test set with bootstrap CIs is the norm, and n = 80 episodes from 28 concepts (iteration 1) is not believed. dAUC from nested logistic models is a known weak test (Pepe et al.; Vickers), so a coefficient or LR test is reported alongside it.\\n(5) MEASURES. AUC, dAUC, pseudo-R2, LR tests and marginal effects for entry and exit. Rarefied or volume-residualised breadth for diffusion. Random-effects meta-analysis across domains with I^2. Spearman or partial Spearman for concept-level associations. Results are reported per field or domain, and in pooled form.\","]}], "userModified": false, "replaceAll": false}

If it is missing, rerun snapshot_meta.py logic on the S3 sources+topics snapshot.\\n  - results/field_names.csv and results/topic_meta.csv: topic -> subfield -> field map.\\n  - scan/matches/*.jsonl: P78 title matches, used ONLY as a regression test for the new matcher.\\n  - results/outcomes.csv: the S0 API t0 of P78 concepts, used as an onset-agreement check.\\n(2) art_33_KKk_G8Gw5 = 3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_4/.\\n  - field_backbone.json: the FROZEN 26-field 1998-2002 positive-PMI backbone.
